# Plugin API matrix — 2026-11-01

This inventory is input to the new plugin-contract ADR required by
[ADR-0250 §9](../content/docs/en/adr/0250-the-runtime-is-a-headless-kernel-the-desktop-embeds.md#9-plugins-a-new-contract-in-one-cutover)
and phase 0a A6; it is not that ADR. The actual catalog is
`packages/plugin-contract/contract/catalog.json`, not the plan's
`packages/plugin-sdk/contract/catalog.json`. Snapshot of commit
`1eb67e1e00` (generator and every input as committed there). This is a pinned
research snapshot, not a gate: later commits may drift from it.

Regenerate: `node scripts/gates/convergence/plugin-api-matrix.mjs --write` (also
writes the per-method JSON, every call site included, to the gitignored
`.cache/convergence/plugin-api-matrix.json`). `--check` compares this note with a
fresh run; without flags the command only prints counts.
Fixtures: `node --test scripts/gates/convergence/plugin-api-matrix.test.mjs`.
Requires installed `typescript` and `python3` (stdlib `ast`); tests use in-memory
files and create no temporary directories. The generator preserves this prose
and owns the sections between the markers. It is separate from the existing
convergence counter because resolving plugin factories, signatures and callers
requires a different source model. Package/registry integration remains with
the worktree owner; this research freshness check is not a blocking gate.

Detection rules:

- **Metadata:** one row per catalog method. `headless` comes exclusively from
  namespace `platforms`; it is a declaration, not runtime acceptance.
  Method-level `runtimes` override namespace defaults.
- **TypeScript provision:** resolve the catalog `factory` on the assembled
  context, following imports/re-exports, aliases, object spreads, nested groups
  and existing guards. Interfaces and unrelated objects do not count. Inspect
  each mounted function's control flow, including delegated helpers, throws,
  rejected promises and tagged refusal results. A member with no success path
  is `no`, with the refusal named; conditional/platform/permission guards with
  success paths remain provided. The complete refusal list is generated below.
  Unresolved object shapes are `unclear`. Structural provision does not prove
  platform availability, permission grants or successful native execution.
- **Python and JSON transport:** require SDK exposure from `ctx.py`/siblings and
  its independently parsed `_generated_contract.py`, a router route, a TS
  implementation, and a transportable signature. Inspect the real author types,
  following type imports and aliases, optional groups, overloads, nested options
  and mapped types. The generated inventory lists every detected callable input
  across all namespaces. Callables in arguments/returns and known non-JSON
  values need a method-specific adapter; without one a Python declaration is
  `declared-only`. Optional callback features also count: a JSON-only subset may
  still work. Explicit SDK/router callback refusals remain `no`; undeclared
  runtimes are `n/a`. The router's AsyncIterable result collapse is recognized.
  Opaque `any`/`unknown` data slots remain subject to the actual JSON frame's
  serialization checks; they are not a promise that arbitrary objects serialize.
  Manifest contribution RPC does not adapt arbitrary `ctx.*` callback arguments.
- **WASM:** imported WIT interfaces only; kebab-case → camelCase, plus
  `process.exec` → `shell.execute` and `secrets.set` → `secrets.store`.
  This is surface equivalence, not ABI parity. Consolidated/different operations
  (`logger.log`, `notification.notify`, `ai.generate-text`, `workflow.emit-event`,
  `process.exec-with-stdin`) have no exact catalog-method equivalent. Equal
  current/frozen coverage does not mean equal signatures or runtime support.
  `check-plugin-sdk-wit.mjs` checks mirrors; it contains no catalog mapping.
- **Layers:** audit `LAYER_RULES` and reasoned overrides. Namespace ownership is
  refined by method effects and selected-client dependencies: active project,
  session, workspace root, editor/selection and route state require a client.
  Implicit selected-project operations and automation's focused-session consent/
  PiP fallback are `both`. Explicit-id data reads (including active collaboration
  for a named document, goals for a named session and host account selection)
  remain host data. This is the ownership split, not a completed kernel migration.
- **Callers and unknowns:** count source call sites once under each first-party
  `plugin.json`, using its real id; exclude tests, dependencies/vendor, build
  output, hidden paths and symlinks. TS AST propagates context and namespace
  provenance through arguments into parameters under any name, imports,
  returned helpers, aliases, destructuring, optional chaining and object slots.
  Python AST handles direct/awaited calls and aliases. Paths use `authorPath`.
  Unresolved namespace/method candidates are retained as
  `firstPartyUnknownCallSites` and counted separately from proven-context
  `firstPartyCallers`; JSON records both sets with locations and reasons.
- **Execution:** Python runs on the host. For TS, activation/deactivation and
  declared plugin contributions establish entries; only actual calls or known
  tool/hook/scheduler/interceptor/standard callback execution contracts propagate
  execution. Merely nesting a closure in `activate` does not execute it. Stored
  escaping closures without proven host execution remain `unclear`, including
  callbacks reached through a view. JSX/view handlers seed client execution.
  `hostSideUiCall` is true for any proven host caller of a UI/`both` method,
  unclear for unresolved context/execution without a proven host caller,
  otherwise false. These calls need ADR-0250's online-client routing even if
  today's adapter rejects them. Execution-unknown sites have a separate count.

Blind spots: this is static source evidence, not invocation frequency or proof
that every source module is loaded. Dynamic method strings/computed keys,
external higher-order APIs and arbitrary class/constructor injection can exceed
this bounded points-to analysis. Unresolved candidates are preserved rather than
silently treated as absent. Context-sensitive branch ordering, arbitrary runtime
mutation and dynamic module loading are not modeled. Zero proven callers does
not authorize API removal. All counts and generated tables come from the same
analysis; none are manually estimated.
